The government, with Minister of Finance Kristin Halvorsen at the head, has carried out a strong tightening of the legislation. As a result, the ‘raw sale’ of these unintended and complex products has been stopped. For example, the information requirement has been reinforced both in the autumn of 2006 and in the new year 2008. A number of investor protection provisions of the Securities Code have been transferred to the sale of such products by financial institutions. It is also the case that, following the implementation of the MiFID Directive, investment advice has become a compulsory service and disclosure requirements have been strengthened. With regard to the credit supervision, it is not as claimed in the notes that the supervision has authorised composite products. The supervision does not carry out a general product review and approval activities. The supervision has been active in the process of tightening up the sales and advisory obligations of financial institutions with regard to the products they sell through regulatory changes and guidelines. The supervision has also focused on so-called non-professional customers in relation to composite products. The work of the credit supervision has clearly led to the cessation of debt financing for such products. The Progress Party makes provision both for comments and proposals to ensure that staff members of the Banking Authority are placed in the banks, in order to improve the interaction between supervision and banks and better information flow, as they say in their comments. I don't think such a scheme would be useful in Norway. Firstly, the supervision of Norwegian banks and other financial institutions by the credit supervision works well. Unlike many other countries, Norway has a comprehensive financial regulation and supervision covering the whole financial sector. Credit supervision is active in the field of local supervision and access to the file of all financial institutions. Internationally, the main challenge is weak regulation and not least fragmented supervision. It has been a significant contribution to our having such a strong international crisis. Today, EU and international processes are taking action to improve financial regulation and supervision of financial institutions. The main challenge is not that there is too poor interaction between the credit supervision and banks in Norway, but that financial markets are international and that many actors operate in many countries. The most important thing is that regulation is not locked to borders, but that we have a comprehensive system that can operate across borders and continents. It is important to arrive at an internationally binding comprehensive regulatory framework.

The same will unfortunately apply to very many children who in today's society have divorced parents. I think most parents have favored their children through private insurance. If one parent goes away and the child is a minor, the child will be paid the sum, but that will be a full tax. That is why I believe that the proposals that have been made by the minority are good proposals – proposals that I would have liked to have been adopted in Stortinget, that we would have gone through this legislation thoroughly to see what the negative consequences of today's tax system actually lead to for the minors we are talking about here. Finally, I have to say that I very much support what the representative Sponheim said in his post. He drew it out with tax planning, and when we know what cases this is about, it's at the limit of the tasteless.
ParlaMint-NO_2009-06-11.ana.ud1232e3625	Several have not asked for the floor on case No. 11th@item: inlistbox [[Fotation, see page 3599]]
ParlaMint-NO_2009-06-11.ana.ud1232e3642	No one has asked for the floor. [[Footing, see page 3600]]
ParlaMint-NO_2009-06-11.ana.ud1232e3659	The proposers focus on one of Norway’s most important and largest district industries. Reference is made to the fact that the construction industry is particularly hard hit by the financial crisis, that it has been 23 years since a growth report on industry was submitted, and that it is therefore important that the challenges for industry are fully presented from the Government through a growth report. The proposers point out that there are many unresolved tasks in infrastructure and public building mass and that nutrient taxation is a heavy burden. And tax reliefs are demanded to get the economic activity back on track. The majority of the Committee – the parties to the government – rejects the proposal. They refer to letters from the Council of State and justify the fact that the construction industry is now approaching a normal situation because the Government has already taken a number of short-term immediate measures to mitigate the effects of the international financial crisis. An overall opposition supports the proposal for a deposit notification. There are very many public bodies that are currently responsible for the public building stock. All departments are, to a greater or lesser extent, ordering new construction services and maintenance, which suggests that the need for an overall strategy of the Government in this area is present. The organisation of management, operation, initial investment and maintenance should be more effective than it is today, thus contributing to greater predictability and long-term sustainability of the construction industry through a large-scale notification of the construction industry. If we ignore the hopefully short-term problems facing the construction industry in these financial crisis times, the Right believes that the industry will face several challenges in the long term. The purpose of a big message is to look far into the future. Therefore, the right considers that, through a potential large-scale communication, a climate-friendly building should also be illuminated and discussed, increased competence in industry, unprofessional operators in industry and, not least, the cessation of black work. Construction errors represent an average of 4 pst. of the value of newly built dwellings after takeover and the Consumer Council receives between 10 000 and 12 000 requests for housing problems. Therefore, the right has previously proposed consumer rights in the case of transfer of property – Document No. 8:62 for 2008-2009 – where we refer to media postings where it appears that only 10–15 pst. of the defects in the construction are discovered before six years have elapsed. Therefore, the right proposed the introduction of a 10-year guarantee period, a service booklet for housing and compulsory construction insurance and a condition report. The right shares the concern about the exceptional situation in which the construction industry is currently located. We have therefore supported the Government’s package of measures to stimulate growth in industry and thus also the construction industry. Nevertheless, we will once again point out that the crisis package from the Government was irresponsibly late. Right-wing promotion of its proposals for the crisis package already in the budget in the autumn of 2008. We are critical that the Government lacks the will to implement what we will describe as more accurate measures, as the Right has made through a number of proposals to counter an increasing number of unemployed through permits and bankruptcies. The right proposes, among other things: removing the Government's tax increases and increasing the depreciation rates for machines from 20 pst. until 25 pst. We propose a stronger investment in road construction than the Government, and we want municipalities to keep a share of the corporate tax to motivate municipalities to facilitate business development. The right also proposed to extend the loan scheme to municipalities from NOK 2 billion to NOK 5 billion to ensure more activity in refurbishment of school buildings, etc., which would have benefited the construction industry to the greatest extent. In the right, it is positive that the proposers propose to shed light on the challenges of the construction industry through a new large-scale communication, and we therefore support the proposal.

So it's like many times being promoted a big message in the period. We have just, today, dealt with the national transport plan, which comes once in a period. There are also other types of messages that come at regular intervals, such as the state of the state of the kingdom and some others. I think it is quite strange in many ways that it is not a total big deal that will have such a big message, simply because there are fluctuations in the industry, and there are also big lags in public building. Only in the municipal sector will 100 billion DKK be invested in the next 10 years. Within school it's $65 billion, and in the case of public hospitals, the investment need is $7 billion every year. That you don't have a more conscious strategy from the Government in that way, I think it's a little amazing, and it's also a little amazing that the government parties can't support us in this matter. But it has become a tradition, unfortunately, that all the proposals that the opposition is promoting are being put to the vote. ParlaMint-NO_2009-06-11.ana.ud1232e3706	The representative of Øyvind Korsberg has taken up the proposal to which he referred.
ParlaMint-NO_2009-06-11.ana.ud1232e3709	I'll just briefly say a few words about why we don't support the proposal. It's simply because when we made the Soria Moria statement, we drew out a lot of industries that we said we should give priority by making our own messages and drawing them out in different ways. It doesn't mean in any way that we don't think the construction industry is important, but we follow the Soria Moria statement in our government period these four years, and then we get back after the election with a new round in different areas. What's important about the construction industry is that it's an industry that's got the feeling that we have a government that wanted to pursue an active policy, that's just the construction industry. We have carried out a lot of dialogue with the construction industry, which has developed the industry, ensuring that the construction industry does not experience downtime, and we have supported the serious parts of the construction industry. It is enough to mention that in these fluctuating business cycle times we have made sure to put a lot of money in, among other things. through the House Bank, which has been important for the construction industry. Had it been up to the Right and Advance Party programmes in the last period, we had not had the House Bank as a counter-cyclical bank to use in these times. We have made sure to introduce new building regulations in cooperation with the construction industry, which have just ensured that our construction industry is one of those that are moving very strongly and in the right direction in terms of the environment and climate. We have provided new legislation on planning legislation, which is important in order for the construction industry to have predictability and long-termity in relation to the plan law. In the context of the crisis package, we have also presented measures relating to depreciation rates for investments made this year, which have been important. We have also taken steps to support the serious part of the construction industry through our policy of publicising collective agreements, by following up on the need to have proper pay and working conditions in the construction industry, which is precisely important to ensure that unprofessional workers are out of business and that the serious ones are able to compete well with others. So the construction industry, we all, both the finance committee, the business committee and the housing committee, etc., have always had close and good cooperation with, to ensure that the best for the construction industry is nourished and in relation to long-term and stability. Therefore, we say that the construction industry is a good industry and an important industry for Norway, but we have not chosen to go in for a separate growth message for the construction industry in this round. We do not support the opposition's proposal, simply because we believe that it is not necessary.
